campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:346: - `sources/16. Backtracking Dalakhar & Kalain.pdf` — Inn of the Dripping Dagger detail, Xanathar death mark, Kalain's letter and tower description, Lord Dagult's papers, animated painting mechanic.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:360: - **Appendix E: Villain Factions** — Faction response team rosters and deployment behavior; Xanathar Guild gazer network; Manshoon parallel investigation; Bregan D'Aerthe conditional activation scaling with party choices at the Faire.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:386: ***The First Arc Where All Four Villain Factions Pursue the Same Object.*** Arc A establishes the Xanathar Guild and Manshoon's Zhentarim as adversaries. Arc B introduces the Cassalanters as patrons and Bregan D'Aerthe as observers. Arc C is the first arc where all four factions reach for the same object at the same time in the same city. The design makes this concrete rather than atmospheric: Xanathar gazers in the alley, Zhent parallel investigation, BD conditional observers scaling with party choices at the Faire, Cassalanter condolence note and dinner invitation. These are not flavor. They are the Grand Game becoming visible for the first time — the moment the party understands they are not the only investigators in Waterdeep, and that the city is full of people who already know more than they do.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:13: The investigation that began in Trollskull Alley ends at the gate of Gralhund Villa on Saerdoun Street, North Ward. Urstul Floxin's Zhentarim strike team is coming on the night of Ches 24th. Xanathar's gazers are already watching the perimeter. Jarlaxle's Bregan D'Aerthe — if the party found their way to him in Arc C — has a team in the house across the street and knows more about what is inside than anyone. Lady Yalah Gralhund has the Stone and is holding it against all of them. The party's window is less than a day.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:27: Floxin killed his two watchers and was gone by the following morning. Two bodies in G9 mark where he was held. Lady Gralhund has the Stone. The nimblewright stands in the corner of her bedroom, repaired and waiting. Xanathar's gazers, which tracked Floxin from the fireball site, are posted at cardinal points around the estate — four of them, watching the exits. Bregan D'Aerthe has a team across Saerdoun Street in the empty Artheyn Manor, watching all of them watch each other. Lady Gralhund does not know the drow are there.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:49: *Jarlaxle Baenre / Captain Zardoz Zord (Bregan D'Aerthe — conditional on Arc C ev-04: Jarlaxle Informed).* Jarlaxle reaches out via a carnival acrobat who delivers a sealed note to wherever the party is staying. The note offers a meeting. In the meeting, he provides the complete nimblewright ledger, a detailed layout of the villa's upper floor from recordings made by the nimblewright's embedded clairvoyance crystal, and specific positions for Xanathar's four gazers on the perimeter. He does not mention that Fel'rekt Lafeen's team is already in the house across the street. His generosity is genuine in content. It is calculated in purpose. He is measuring whether the party can be trusted with real intelligence — and whether they are the kind of people who notice what was not offered. Cross-ref Arc C Design Note on "investigation quality has consequences."
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:71: **Zhentarim Watchers (DC 12 Perception).** Two Zhent thugs on nearby rooftops, one black flying snake courier between them. They are watching the villa to ensure no one leaves before Floxin's team arrives. They noticed one of Xanathar's gazers several days ago but could not identify it. They are unaware of Jarlaxle's team in Artheyn Manor. If they spot the party approaching: the flying snake carries word to Floxin. His strike team mobilizes and arrives in 10 + 2d6 minutes. The party has that window.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:73: **Xanathar's Gazers (DC 16 Perception).** Four gazers positioned at cardinal points around the villa. Each PC success on the Perception check reveals one; each additional 2 points of margin reveals another. The gazers followed Floxin to the villa and are installed to understand what he wants from the Gralhunds. They do not intervene in anything that happens at the villa itself — they report and follow. If the party is spotted: no immediate reaction. Xanathar now knows the party is involved. The gazers will follow anyone who leaves the villa with something valuable.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:257: *Zhentarim Failure Is Not Foreordained.* If the party draws off or eliminates the Barracks reinforcements (beat 9), Floxin's team may actually win the ground floor and hold it. If Jarlaxle's team enters early — triggered by PC action rather than the natural timeline — the sequence collapses into improvisation. Xanathar's gazers observe throughout and will not intervene inside the estate.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:297: *Xanathar's Gazer Intervention.* Xanathar's gazers, which have been following anyone who left the villa with something valuable, may intercede in the chase. The DM chooses one of two outcomes:
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:299: - **Tactical assist:** A gazer swoops in and fires a paralytic ray at whoever is carrying the Stone, crippling them for one round and letting the party close distance. Xanathar would rather the party have the Stone than any other faction — he can always take it from the party later.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:300: - **Response Team intercept:** If the DM wants a harder version of this scene, a Xanathar Guild response team (pre-positioned in the Dock Ward) cuts off the rooftop exit route, producing a three-way confrontation for the Stone. Cross-ref Appendix E response team rosters.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:302: *If the Chase Fails.* The Stone reaches a Bregan D'Aerthe outpost (Fenerus Stormcastle in the Trades Ward), a Zhentarim safe house, or is carried back to Xanathar — depending on who took it. From there it moves to the faction's primary lair within 2d6 days. The party has not lost the campaign; they have lost their current leverage and must find a new approach to the faction now holding it. Cross-ref Appendix E outpost and lair entries for which faction ends up with the Stone and what that implies for the next arc.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:339: - **Lords' Alliance (Jalester):** His debrief request arrives within twenty-four hours. The Watch officers on the Saerdoun Street beat were transferred to a Far South Ward assignment the week before the raid — a reassignment he has traced to a request from inside the Watch hierarchy, source unknown. He wants to know what the party saw inside the villa, and whether there was any evidence of Watch coordination with the Xanathar Guild or the Gralhund household.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:340: - **Emerald Enclave (Melannor):** Gralhund Villa sits over a minor ley line the Enclave monitors as part of Jeryth's ongoing aberrant-activity survey. The Xanathar Guild's presence in the neighborhood destabilized it over the past week in a way that correlates with Nihiloor's movements below the city. Melannor wants to know what happened inside. Not for political reasons. For the map.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:342: - **Force Grey (Vajra):** Vajra wants a full operational debrief — which factions were present, where each one entered and exited, whether any Watch checkpoint in the surrounding blocks was compromised or unmanned during the raid, and any direct evidence of Nihiloor's involvement. Xanathar Guild activity in close proximity to a Watch checkpoint the Guild has never previously approached is, in her judgment, a test. She wants to know if the test succeeded.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:423: - **Appendix E: Villain Factions** — Manshoon's Weave motivation and blackmailed Masked Lords (Corylus Thann, Jelenn Urmbrusk); *A Directive from Jarlaxle* document listing; *Gralhund Study of the Grand Game* document listing; Yellowspire section (Chirada, Second Eye, teleportal); Xanathar Guild response teams (Scene 5 gazer intercept option).
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:455: The intelligence Jarlaxle offers in Scene 1 is completely accurate. He has the ledger. He has the villa layout. He knows where Xanathar's gazers are positioned. He gives the party all of it, freely, without apparent agenda.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:471: If the chase fails — if the Stone reaches a Bregan D'Aerthe outpost, a Zhentarim safe house, or Xanathar's custody — the campaign continues, but the party enters Arc E negotiating from weakness rather than strength. Jarlaxle will deal with them. Xanathar will not (Xanathar never deals). Manshoon's cell will know the party is active and has failed. These are real consequences that reshape the social landscape of the subsequent arcs without ending the campaign. The Stone is not irretrievable — but retrieving it from a faction lair is Arc E's problem, not Arc D's.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:485: The Watch's sweep removes Davil from play, puts Tashlyn in a provisional leadership role she has not asked for, and signals to every faction in the city that the Grand Game has become visible to law enforcement. Manshoon's cell goes quieter. Xanathar's operations in the North Ward become more careful. Jarlaxle's team leaves no evidence at Artheyn Manor and is never definitively connected to the villa's events. The party will be questioned if they were seen. Their choices during the crackdown — who they protect, who they hand to the Watch, how they handle Istrid Horn — begin to define their faction relationships in the second half of the campaign.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:15: Ten outposts are distributed across Waterdeep's wards, maintained by four factions, each one a data point in the web of the Grand Game. The party cannot storm the lairs blind — Xanathar's sanctum, Cassalanter Villa's temple, the Sea Maidens Faire, and Kolat Towers are not targets until the party knows enough to attempt them. The outposts are the research: entering a Xanatharian front to find how the Guild accesses its own lair, or breaking into a cultist's apartment to find a map the Cassalanters spent months assembling.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:35: That calculation is no longer accurate. Neverember is in Neverwinter. The parties holding his Eyes have been in Waterdeep long enough to develop their own plans for whatever the Stone might reveal. Jarlaxle Baenre holds his Eye aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth*, where it serves as leverage and curiosity in equal measure. The Cassalanter family, working through a network of cult agents and infernal diviners, located a second Eye buried in the crypt of a noblewoman who died without heirs, and extracted it. Xanathar holds the last in Sylgar's fishbowl in the lair beneath Skullport. Zhentarim envoys stole it from the Protector's Enclave in Neverwinter and carried it to Xanathar as a gift. Xanathar killed them, kept the stone, and has never learned what it is.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:37: Manshoon holds no Eye. His envoys carried one to Xanathar and never came back. He wants the Stone itself — specifically, the compressed aboleth memory within it, which encodes structural knowledge of the Weave that Mystra's servants have never catalogued. The vault is an instrument he would use to fund his actual goal. The Stone is the goal. He needs the Stone and any two Eyes, but he never intended to open the vault the way Neverember did — only to hold the Stone long enough to extract what he needs.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:39: The Stone now sits with the party. The Watch crackdown following the Gralhund Villa incident has disrupted Manshoon's Brindul Alley operation, scattered the Zhentarim response teams, and landed Davil Starsong in Watch custody on circumstantial evidence. Xanathar's Guild is consolidating — pulling agents tighter, increasing surveillance. Jarlaxle watches the party with quiet interest and no urgency; he holds something they need, and he is in no hurry to discuss terms. The Cassalanters, undisturbed, continue their research. Below the city's peaceful surface, four factions know the Stone has surfaced. Three of them want it back.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:63: *Stone Knowledge Expansion (Forward Note — Implement in Heist Arcs).* The Alexandrian Remix establishes that the Stone's knowledge expands incrementally as each Eye is restored. With zero Eyes, it yields only the fragmentary impressions above. With one Eye, it reveals additional details. With two, more still. All three Eyes unlock the vault's precise location and command phrase. This expansion belongs in the lair heists (**Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**) where Eyes are actually recovered — not in this quest. This note exists so the mechanic is not overlooked when those arcs are drafted. Cross-ref `sources/8. The Eyes of the Stone.pdf` for the authoritative expansion table.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:81: *Mirt (Harpers — renown 1+).* The Harpers have been watching the Cassalanter family for years, and Mirt's summary reflects sustained, inconclusive surveillance. The Cassalanters give more to charity than houses twice their wealth, never miss a temple service, and have not made a single political enemy in a decade. "Nobody is that clean," Mirt says, without pleasure. He does not know what they are hiding. The Harpers have found no evidence of wrongdoing — only the complete absence of any wrongdoing at all, which in Waterdeep's political ecology is itself suspicious. He recommends starting with Xanathar outposts on the grounds that the Guild's paranoia makes early strikes more effective than late ones. His unstated reason: the Harpers have already exhausted their Xanathar intelligence, and the Cassalanter question is the one he cannot answer and cannot let go.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:115: **Xanathar's Guild:** Grinda Garloth's operates openly in Mistshore as a black-market armorer. Anyone asking about Xanathar weapons or Guild suppliers in the Dock Ward gets pointed to her within a day. No credentials required.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:127: *Cross-Faction Spillover.* Xanathar and Manshoon are actively at war; each monitors the other's losses. Hitting a Xanathar outpost raises Manshoon's alert tier by 1, and vice versa — both factions assume the other is responsible before they conclude it was the party. Cassalanter imp surveillance of the Sea Maidens Faire creates a reciprocal intelligence channel; Cassalanter losses alert Jarlaxle that something is moving against infernal-adjacent operations in the city. See **Running the Villains** (GM Guide) for full inter-faction dynamics.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:135: ### Scene 4: Xanathar's Guild Outposts
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:137: **Purpose:** Present three Xanathar outposts as a linked intelligence chain, each one peeling back a layer of organized crime — a gentleman's clinic, an arms dealership, a confectioners' front over a duergar smuggling cellar. All three feed into **Xanathar's Lair**.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:147: *Step 1 — Gather Information.* Three paths to Terasse Estate before the party enters it: the *Instructions for Gladiatorial Weaponry* found at Grinda Garloth's (names Terasse as a delivery point for exotic arms); the mechanical beholder letter at Orb Confectioners (names Terasse as the delivery address and dates it); or, if **Cassalanter Villa** has been completed first, a Cassalanter Villa report referencing the gladiatorial supply chain. Parties lacking these documents can attempt entry by bluffing past the door with the healing passphrase (DC 18 Deception if improvising) or by obtaining the passphrase from an interrogated Xanathar response team member.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:149: *Step 2 — Case the Target.* A modest, unremarkable rowhouse. Two kinds of visitors come: those seeking healing (legitimate front), and those attending gladiatorial events in Xanathar's Lair. Two passphrases control entry: "I heard the red hand was hot" accesses the healing operation; "the bawdy star shines bright tonight" gets a guest past the front rooms. See the **Xanathar's Guild** Factions guide page for the full area layout.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:153: *Step 4 — The Operation.* Primary objectives: recover the *Instructions for Gladiatorial Weaponry* from Keln's desk (Area 6–7), which names Grinda Garloth as the supplier and Ahmaergo as the authorizing agent; recover the *Injury Reports* logbook (Area 5 Upper Landing), which contains an entry noting a wound caused by "incautious observation of the windmill on Coachlamp Lane" — a cross-faction clue pointing toward the Cassalanter outpost chain; recover the *Ill Man's Note* (Area 8–10 Sick Rooms), which contains the Orb Confectioners passphrase in verse. Optional: follow a guest being transported to the gladiatorial event. The trap door in the kitchen leads to an underground dock; guests are blindfolded before descent and transported by boat to Area X4 of Xanathar's Lair. This route is a genuine lair entry method, but arriving via gladiatorial transport means arriving unprepared, blindfolded, and unarmed. See the **Xanathar's Guild** Factions guide page for keyed area details.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:155: *Step 5 — Getaway.* Keln Trigos (priest) and his guards raise the alarm when any objective area is breached: the trap door seals from below, the front door bars, and a flying snake courier carries word to the Lair. The alarm window is 1d4+2 rounds before lockdown. **Escalation: Xanathar moves to Suspicious (Tier 2).** If a second Xanathar outpost has already been hit, Xanathar moves directly to Alert (Tier 3) and Gorath's response team deploys to the last known party location within 2 days.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:163: *Dock Ward, Mistshore.* — **Street-level entry point for the Xanathar chain.**
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:165: *Approach.* Grinda operates openly as a black-market armorer. Anyone asking about Xanathar weapons, Guild suppliers, or black-market arms in the Dock Ward gets pointed to Mistshore within a day (DC 12 Charisma in Dock Ward taverns or street contacts; no faction membership required). Three additional paths: the *Instructions for Gladiatorial Weaponry* from Terasse Estate names her; a captured Xanathar response team member's *Directive from Nar'l* names her as a supplier; Agorn Fuoco's *Report to Agorn Fuoco* (from any Zhentarim response team) confirms her location. Parties can approach as customers, investigators, or raiders. Grinda does not fight for the Guild — she fights for her business. She will negotiate if the party offers more immediate value than Xanathar does, and she will report the encounter afterward.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:167: *Key Areas.* Area D2: 500 gp and the *Note Regarding Gladiatorial Weaponry* from Ahmaergo, confirming delivery to Terasse Estate and citing Xanathar's expectation of "exotic gutting." Area D3: mundane arms and armor including single-shot pistols; 1d4 pieces of magic arms or armor (DMG Table F); 1d4 miscellaneous magic items (DMG Table A or B). See the **Xanathar's Guild** Factions guide page for full area descriptions.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:169: *Intel Yield.* The Ahmaergo correspondence confirms the Terasse connection and establishes Ahmaergo as a named Guild lieutenant. Grinda's knowledge of sewer patrol routes is available through negotiation or by locating her operational logbook — partial maps of Xanathar's Lair approach corridors. The magic item inventory is genuine loot.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:171: **Escalation: Xanathar moves to Suspicious (Tier 2) if this outpost is raided; no tier change if the party engages as customers without taking documents.** Response teams do not deploy from a single customer interaction. **Founders' Day Clock: 1–2 days.**
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:175: #### 4C: Orb Confectioners / Xanathar Sewer Hideout (Encounter-Plus)
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:181: *Approach.* Three paths to Orb Confectioners: a captured Xanathar response team member carries the passphrase or the location in their directive; Agorn Fuoco's *Report to Agorn Fuoco* names Orb Confectioners on Rising Ride; Terasse Estate's *Ill Man's Note* contains the passphrase in verse. The passphrase exchange: ask for "eight small eyes." The counter-staff responds: "How would you like those arranged?" Answer: "Around an ocular cake." Correct answers earn an escort to the cellar stairs. Use the Autumn Cellar Complex layout (DH p. 86). See the **Xanathar's Guild** organization page.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:183: *Key Areas.* Area B7: *On the Delivery of a Mechanical Beholder* from Ahmaergo, setting a delivery deadline of Tarsakh 9th for a mechanical beholder to Terasse Estate — confirms the gladiatorial supply chain and ties Ahmaergo to Xanathar's engineering operations. Area B9b: a **mimic** disguised as the chest at the foot of Korgstrod's bed. A hidden coffer under the bed (DC 13 to find) holds 500 gp and *Correspondence with Nar'l Xibrindas*: Nar'l's letter references smokepowder and provides guildsign directions toward Area X1 of Xanathar's Lair. The guildsign trail requires DC 15 checks, rising to DC 17 near the lair itself; three failures before a success means the trail is lost. See the **Xanathar's Guild** organization page.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:185: *Intel Yield.* Nar'l's correspondence is the primary entry path to Xanathar's Lair via the guildsign trail. The smokepowder reference seeds Nar'l's double-agent status — he is also in contact with Jarlaxle, a fact that pays off in **Xanathar's Lair** and **Sea Maidens Faire** when the BD-Xanathar connection surfaces.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:187: **Escalation: Xanathar moves to Alert (Tier 3) if this outpost is cleared.** Korgstrod's response — invisibility, enlargement, and four duergar fighting to the death — signals this location is more important than the others. Response teams deploy within 1d4 days of the operation. **Founders' Day Clock: 1–3 days.**
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:209: *Ott Steeltoes.* A gnome prisoner wearing a leather skullcap stitched with fake beholder eyestalks — an Xanatharian agent captured during the Guild war. Ott does not know what the Stone of Golorr is, but he knows Xanathar's Lair. If rescued and cultivated, he offers to introduce the party to the Guild or guide them personally to the lair's entrance. If freed without follow-through, he disappears back into Xanathar's network. See the **Ott Steeltoes** page in Notable Figures and the **Xanathar's Guild** organization page.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:211: *Intel Yield.* Manshoon's name (first use in the campaign). The pass-amulet from Avareen (route to Kolat Towers). Ott Steeltoes as a potential **Xanathar's Lair** contact. The *Directive to Zorbog* naming Fenerus Stormcastle — a cross-faction thread pointing toward Bregan D'aerthe (Fenerus is a BD lamplighter; Manshoon has identified him as a soft target).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:213: **Escalation: Manshoon moves to Alert (Tier 3).** Avareen's flying snakes carry the alarm faster than the party can move; if even one escapes, Manshoon knows within hours. Vevette Blackwater's team deploys within 1d4 days. If Xanathar cross-faction spillover applies, Manshoon moves directly to Lockdown (Tier 4). **Founders' Day Clock: 1–2 days.**
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:327: *Step 1 — Gather Information.* Four paths to the theater as a BD outpost rather than a public venue: Fenerus's decoded messages (theater play titles serve as coded correspondence; one decoded message lists a stage address resolving to a dressing room number); a Xanathar's Lair report naming the Seven Masks Theater as a BD surface operation (available after **Xanathar's Lair**); Soluun Xibrindas's *Bloodstained Playbill* recovered from a BD response team, marked with a seat number pointing to the safehouse dressing room; or public theater investigation — noticing that one dressing room is never listed on the cast sheet.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:351: *Response Team Compositions.* See the relevant Factions guide pages for full compositions and carried documents. Every team leader carries faction-specific documents that function as intelligence opportunities: Zhentarim teams carry directives naming Brindul Alley or Kolat Towers; Xanathar teams carry operational correspondence from Nar'l or Ahmaergo; BD teams carry playbills or coded messages in Drow; Cassalanter teams carry instructions from Lord Cassalanter. The party should treat every response team encounter as a potential document recovery, not just a combat encounter.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:353: *Cross-Faction Response Team Encounters.* Xanathar and Manshoon teams may encounter each other during this quest — both factions are moving through the same wards. Witnessing these confrontations without engaging either side generates intelligence about both factions simultaneously. BD teams prefer misdirection over lethal force; they are more likely to shadow the party than confront them directly. Cassalanter teams vary by the party's relationship with the family.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:355: *The Cassalanter Dual Mode.* The Cassalanters respond differently depending on whether the party has accepted Ammalia's **Fireball!** dinner invitation and maintained a working relationship. Working with the Cassalanters: Seffia's team assists or at minimum does not interfere. Opposing or refusing them: imps maintain constant surveillance, Seffia's team competes at **Xanathar's Lair** operations, and Willifort Crowelle makes social approaches that escalate to assassination attempts if the party actively works against the family. See the **Cassalanters** Factions guide page (Response Teams section).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:369: *Mirt (Harpers).* Full intelligence briefing. If Xanathar outposts were hit, Mirt asks specifically about Nihiloor references — the Harper file on the mind flayer is extensive, and any new document strengthens the case for Watch action. If Cassalanter outposts were hit and the party brings the Mirror of the Fiend sigil identification, the Cassalanter cult documents, or the cultists' testimony, Mirt's response is: relief, then urgency. He has been building suspicion for years without evidence. What the party has found in one outpost run is more concrete than everything he gathered in three years of surveillance. "I knew it couldn't just be charity." He does not have enough to move on the Cassalanters legally — he needs **Cassalanter Villa** for that — but the calculus on Founders' Day has changed.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:393: *Completing a chain:* Hit at least two outposts of the same faction and recover at least one piece of actionable lair intelligence. For Xanathar (three outposts), two of three is sufficient. For all other factions (two outposts), both must be cleared. A party that hits one Xanathar outpost and one Zhentarim outpost has started two chains but completed neither.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:395: Total **Faction Outposts**: 1 Milestone Point, for the first completed chain; a second chain earns intelligence, not points. The party remains 4th level through all of **Faction Outposts**, at 10 cumulative points. A party can attempt any lair heist (**Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, or **Kolat Towers**) after completing just one chain, or even none; this quest's outposts are the recommended on-ramp, not a hard gate. See the Milestone section in **Xanathar's Lair** (or any lair heist's) for how the heist phase carries the party from 4th to 7th level.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:397: *Transition.* The party holds enough intelligence to attempt at least one lair heist. The GM reviews each faction's escalation tier and communicates it through environmental detail: Dock Ward patrols are heavier than last tenday; a Castle Ward merchant notes that the old tower on the hill has gone dark; the Sea Maidens Faire hasn't performed in a week. Party chooses which lair first. **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, and **Kolat Towers** are available in any order.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:421: 9. **Chain completion defined.** Two outposts of the same faction plus one piece of actionable lair intelligence. The first completed chain awards 1 Milestone Point (cumulative 10), not enough to reach level 5. Level 5 is reached on the first completed lair heist (**Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, or **Kolat Towers**).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:449: - **Xanathar's Lair** — Xanathar outpost chain (Scene 4) feeds directly into **Xanathar's Lair**. Nar'l's guildsign trail, the gladiatorial transport route, and Ott Steeltoes all function as **Xanathar's Lair** entry methods.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:461: ***The Problem of Non-Linear Content in a Linear Format.*** Every quest through **Gralhund Villa** moved in a single investigative current — a question asked, an investigation run, a confrontation resolved. **Faction Outposts** cannot function this way. Ten outposts in any order, at the party's discretion, cannot be scripted into a sequence without defeating the point. The nine-scene architecture is the solution: three framing scenes that run in order (Stone attunement, faction consultation, operational briefing), four faction sections that function as a menu, and two closing scenes that apply after the menu has been used. The faction sections are labeled as scenes to maintain the document's structural vocabulary — each has a Purpose, Content, and Tone — but the GM should understand that "Scene 4" does not mean "fourth in sequence." It means "this is the Xanathar section."
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:469: ***Cross-Faction Spillover and the Xanathar–Manshoon War.*** The spillover rules — hitting Xanathar raises Manshoon's tier and vice versa — have a specific purpose beyond realism. They ensure that a party pursuing only one faction's chain still generates meaningful pressure across multiple fronts. A party that focuses entirely on Xanathar outposts will encounter Manshoon response teams without having engaged Manshoon directly, surfacing the inter-faction war as lived experience rather than exposition.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:491: ***Lair Entrance Verification.*** Four lair entrances, all reachable from this quest's intelligence: Xanathar's Lair — Nar'l's guildsign trail, gladiatorial transport via Terasse, Ott Steeltoes as guide (three paths); Kolat Towers — Yellowspire teleportation circle, captured pass-amulet from Avareen, Agorn's amulet traded under duress (three paths); Cassalanter Villa — Ammalia's **Fireball!** dinner invitation, Cassalanter cultist testimony from this quest's outposts, Esvele Rosznar in **Cassalanter Villa** (three paths, the last requiring no completion of this quest at all); Sea Maidens Faire — Fenerus's decoded messages, Malcolm Brizzenbright's testimony, Xanathar's Lair report from **Xanathar's Lair** (three paths).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:495: **Harpers.** This quest's Harper hook is an ongoing operative benefit rather than a single scene beat. Once the party reaches Renown 30+, Mirt can direct embedded Harper informants to provide advance intelligence against Xanathar and Manshoon outposts — shift rotations, guard counts, unusual personnel — delivered before each operation rather than discovered during it. A separate informant inside the Sea Maidens Faire reports that Jarlaxle has been asking about the party by description; Mirt passes this to Harper PCs as a quiet heads-up in the days before **Sea Maidens Faire** begins. These benefits are not surfaced in Scene 2's consultation; they activate through maintained renown and are delivered as one informant report at the start of each outpost operation the party undertakes in Scenes 4–7, once a Harper PC has Renown 30+. See the Quest Hooks section of the **Harpers** Factions guide page.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:1: # Xanathar's Lair
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:9: Three months before the Trollskull fireball, Manshoon dispatched two Zhentarim lieutenants to Skullport carrying a gift: Eye #1 of the Stone of Golorr, stolen from the Protector's Enclave in Neverwinter. The Eye was offered as a peace overture — proof that Manshoon's splinter faction could be a valuable partner in the Grand Game. Xanathar received the envoys in its throne room, listened to their proposal, and ate them. It kept the Eye and has never learned what it is. The Eye passed to Ott Steeltoes, who placed it in Sylgar's fishbowl because it had a pretty color and Xanathar liked Sylgar's bowl to look interesting.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:11: The lair beneath Skullport has been sealed for weeks. Since the fireball disrupted the Street of Silver trade routes, Xanathar has withdrawn into paranoia — convinced that one of the other Grand Game factions has mapped the lair's entrances. Ahmaergo runs external operations while Xanathar broods in the Sanctum, running its eye rays across the empty room and interrogating Guild members whose faces it does not immediately recognize. The gladiatorial games continue because the arena revenue continues, and because stopping them would signal weakness. But Xanathar does not attend from its throne as it once did. It watches from invisibility, alone.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:13: Nihiloor has used this paranoia as cover. The illithid's three unauthorized projects — running in parallel for years — are now approaching critical phases. The Infiltration: intellect devourers inserted into four positions in Waterdeep's Watch and civic administration, with two additional placements pending. The Experiments: twelve captives from the gladiatorial arena modified into psionic relay organisms, intended to form the backbone of a city-wide intelligence network that would answer only to Nihiloor. The Puppet: four months ago, Nihiloor successfully applied Dominate Monster to Xanathar through a sustained psionic relay device in X26. The Xanathar who has been running the Guild since then — the Xanathar who killed Manshoon's envoys, who sent response teams against the party's allies in **Faction Outposts**, who holds the Eye in a fishbowl because it trusts Ott's aesthetic judgment — has been Nihiloor's instrument. The beholder does not know this. The domination does not feel like domination; it feels like its own decisions.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:15: The panopticus surveillance network — five ghostly beholder eyestalks wired through enchanted crystal to a monitoring station at X16 — was Nihiloor's gift to Xanathar three years ago. The beholder believes the network serves its paranoia. Nihiloor built a structural back door into the control array from the beginning.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:23: **Purpose.** The party holds the Stone of Golorr and knows an Eye is somewhere in Xanathar's lair; that much was established in **Faction Outposts**. This scene converts that knowledge into operational intelligence. Seven faction contacts provide lair-specific information, each from a different angle on the same target. By the scene's end, the party has at least one confirmed entry method, two potential inside contacts, and enough threat assessment to plan an approach.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:31: *Mirt (Harpers — renown 1+).* Thorvin Twinbeard is inside the lair — a Harper asset embedded in the lair's engineering staff for six years, Mirt's most carefully kept secret. Mirt provides Thorvin's location (X13, the workshop) and a recognition code: ask him what he thinks of the new pulley design. His response — "It'll hold if the load is distributed right" — confirms the channel. If Xanathar outposts were hit in **Faction Outposts**, Mirt warns that Thorvin's cover may be compromised: a gazer has been assigned to watch him, and Thorvin knows it. At renown 10+, Mirt arranges a Harper field agent — use the spy stat block — to stage a timed surface distraction above the guildsign entrance: a staged brawl that occupies Guild topside watchers for 20 minutes at a prearranged signal.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:51: **Purpose.** Steps 1 through 3 of the heist framework: Identify the Score, Gather Information, Onsite Surveillance. The party transitions from intelligence briefing to direct reconnaissance of the lair. This scene establishes the three entry methods, maps the panopticus challenge, and forces the party to decide how to handle Xanathar's location before the operation begins.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:55: *Identifying the Score.* The Stone of Golorr's impression — a beholder's eye, unblinking in darkness, carved stone wet from underground springs, a long way down — combined with **Faction Outposts** outpost documents confirms the Eye's location. Xanathar's lair beneath Skullport. The party needs to determine how to enter, how to navigate the surveillance network, where the Eye is held inside, and where Xanathar will be during the operation.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:61: **Gladiatorial Transport (via Terasse Estate).** Discovered through the Terasse Estate mini-heist in **Faction Outposts**. The trap door at Terasse leads to an underground dock where guests are blindfolded and transported by boat to Area X4, arriving as spectators during the gladiatorial games. Advantage: legitimate spectator cover. The crowd and noise provide concealment; DC checks to remain unnoticed in spectator areas drop by 3 during active fights. The party can use tournament sessions to observe NPC movement patterns and confirm Xanathar's location. Disadvantage: the party arrives unarmed. Weapons and obviously magical items are confiscated at X4. The party must smuggle equipment (DC 16 Dexterity (Sleight of Hand) per item) or recover it from Area X9, the equipment cache. If the party's escalation tier is Alert or higher, the tournament may be suspended — this entry method loses its crowd cover and the party arrives into a searched, guarded X4.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:75: *Xanathar's Location.* Xanathar is in X19 (the Sanctum) with Sylgar. It has barely left for weeks. The Eye is there. So is Xanathar.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:77: This means the party cannot simply walk into X19 and take the Eye — not unless they create a window. The dream nullifier in X20 exists for this purpose: destroying it sends Xanathar into the corridor to find Ahmaergo, giving the party approximately 4 minutes. A party that found X20 through reconnaissance or Thorvin's guidance has a clean extraction opportunity. A party that didn't must extract the Eye while a paranoid beholder is in the room.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:95: If the gazer is destroyed (13 HP, AC 13), Thorvin speaks openly for the first time. His relief is immediate and genuine. He confirms what the party may have begun to suspect: Nihiloor's domain (X23–X27) contains evidence that would change everything the party thinks they know about who has been running Xanathar's Guild.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:99: *Inside Ally: Nar'l Xibrindas (X35).* The only drow in the lair, which Xanathar considers proof of its own cosmopolitan generosity. Nar'l's grell bodyguard carries a kill-on-disloyalty order from Xanathar, an instruction the grell will carry out without hesitation. Nar'l cannot dismiss it. He can only avoid triggering it.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:103: With BD allegiance revealed: Nar'l reassesses, then cooperates warily. He can suppress the alarm signal for a specific area for up to ten minutes (logged as a maintenance calibration), redirect a guard patrol once per session, and confirm Xanathar's current location. He will not act against the grell. He will sacrifice the party if doing so is the only way to preserve his cover.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:105: If a party member is BD: Nar'l recognizes them as colleagues and cooperates fully — no hesitation, no wary reassessment. He shares the complete guard rotation, confirms Xanathar's location in X19 and the dream nullifier's purpose, and actively manages alarms rather than offering a single suppression. He also reveals the existence of X14 (the secret passage to X19) if the party has not already found it. The grell constraint still applies: he will not act against it regardless of orders, because the grell is Xanathar's mechanism, not Jarlaxle's. He will evacuate immediately if the smokepowder option is chosen. One additional consequence of BD membership: the Jarlaxle simultaneous heist does not fire. Jarlaxle knew the party was BD, trusted them to handle it, and is waiting for Nar'l's report rather than sending a competing team.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:111: Consequences: Xanathar is destroyed if present in the lair, or escapes through the Undermountain connection at X4 if the GM wants to preserve the thread. The Guild collapses into factional fighting between Ahmaergo and surviving lieutenants. Nar'l and Thorvin must be evacuated or they die. Thorvin will not leave the lair's surviving workers; Nar'l will leave without hesitation. Nihiloor was not in his domain at the time and escapes to Undermountain Level 17 with his three projects intact.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:114: *The Dream Nullifier (X20).* A copper bowl the size of a serving dish, mounted in a maintenance alcove between X19 and X21. A Passive Perception score of 15 or higher notices it while passing through the corridor. A DC 13 Intelligence (Arcana) check identifies its function: it prevents Xanathar from dreaming of other beholders, waking it before the dream takes hold.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:116: Destroying it (antimagic field or dispel magic against DC 15) triggers behavioral change within 3 minutes: intrusive dream-images of other beholders crowd Xanathar's perception. It leaves X19 to find Ahmaergo, demanding explanation, seeking confirmation that the lair is still secure. It moves toward X18 or X12. X19 is empty. The party has approximately 4 minutes.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:123: | Suspicious | X1 unwatched; X4 checked | DC checks +2 | Extra pairs at X2, X4 | Runs; Xanathar remains in X19 |
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:147: **X7 — Cell Block.** Eight cells cut into rock, three occupied. Samara (gladiatorial combatant, six weeks in captivity) knows: Xanathar holds "a magic stone and a marble with a colored center"; the marble is in "the fish tank in the big room with the purple carpet"; and Manshoon's organization is based in a tower in the North Ward (overheard from another prisoner since sent to the arena). The tower information is one of three clue paths to **Kolat Towers**. Two Guild Soldiers watch the cell gate; a third is asleep in a chair. Cell locks are DC 13 Dexterity (Thieves' Tools). If all three prisoners are freed, the two Guild members in custody offer to start a fight at X2 — buying the party 5 minutes before being subdued.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:149: **X12 — Ahmaergo's Quarters.** Organized with obsessive precision: fight records, tournament schedules, arena financial ledgers, a battered sparring sword from Baldur's Gate. On the desk: *Briefing for Xanathar Concerning the Grand Game*. The document covers the current state of the Stone competition — intelligence on the Cassalanters, the Bregan D'aerthe (incomplete), and a physical description of the party accurate enough to confirm they've been watched. Ahmaergo knows who the party is.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:157: **X19 — Xanathar's Sanctum.** A circular room with purple carpet. Personal relics on shelves: small figures, collected baubles, trinkets from significant moments in Xanathar's life. At the center, Sylgar's bowl — a large crystal sphere filled with salt water, algae, decorative rocks, and Sylgar the goldfish. At the bottom of the sphere, among the rocks, glinting faintly through the water's refractions: Eye #1. A small smoky-gray gemstone Ott Steeltoes placed there months ago because it looked like it belonged.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:159: Xanathar is here. The party must extract the Eye with a paranoid beholder in the room — unless they have already used X20.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:161: Extraction without disturbing Sylgar: the sphere has a narrow top opening. DC 12 Dexterity (Sleight of Hand) to remove a stone from the bottom without startling the fish. On a failure, Sylgar thrashes; the water splashes audibly. Xanathar snaps to attention: "What happened to Sylgar? What did you do to Sylgar?" If the fish is harmed — even accidentally — Xanathar becomes Hostile regardless of any prior rapport and does not negotiate. It begins with eye rays.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:163: **X20 — Dream Nullifier.** A copper bowl in a maintenance alcove between X19 and X21. Passive Perception 15 to notice while passing. Destroying it: antimagic field or dispel magic (DC 15). Effect: within 3 minutes, Xanathar experiences intrusive beholder-images and leaves X19 in agitation to find Ahmaergo — demanding explanation, seeking confirmation the lair is secure. It moves toward X18 or X12. X19 is empty. The party has approximately 4 minutes.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:165: **X28 — Guild Recreation Hall.** No scrying sensor. Card games, arm wrestling, complaints about Xanathar. Off-duty Guild members here will not scrutinize strangers with a plausible story. The party can rest up to 1 hour, plan next steps, or wait out a patrol sweep.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:167: **X33 — Crypt of Xanathar's Past.** Through the secret door from X6 (DC 17 Perception), a narrow passage leads to a chamber containing the preserved remains and personal effects of every previous beholder who held the title "Xanathar" — predecessors stretching back centuries. Nar'l Xibrindas is the only current Guild member who knows this room exists. The room contains: evidence that "Xanathar" is a title claimed through murder, not inherited; the original Guild charter signed by the first holder; and locked coffer containing the personal journals of the last three Xanathars, including early dealings with Manshoon's predecessors. The revelation that Xanathar is a title implies the current one could be replaced, and that someone may already be planning to do so.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:169: **X35 — Nar'l's Office.** Sparse and organized by a mind that trusts nothing to memory when paper will do. The desk holds Guild operational reports and a coded communication log in Jarlaxle's cipher (DC 16 Intelligence to decode without the key). In a false-bottomed drawer (DC 14 Intelligence (Investigation)): the *Report on the Seven Masks Theater* — a summary of Jarlaxle's operational activity through the theater's ownership chain, its connection to the Sea Maidens Faire, and a partial decoding of Jarlaxle's Grand Game position. Nar'l has kept this from Xanathar for eleven months. Finding it is one of three clue paths to **Sea Maidens Faire**.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:178: - **X26 — The Puppet.** A psionic relay device, currently active, maintaining a Dominate Monster effect on a psychic signature that matches Xanathar's. Detailed notes track behavioral corrections applied over four months: which tactical decisions were changed, which Guild members were elevated or removed at Nihiloor's direction, and the long-term strategic repositioning of the Guild toward Nihiloor's actual goals. The beholder who rules Xanathar's Guild does not rule Xanathar's Guild. Every Xanathar decision the party has encountered was Nihiloor's decision.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:182: Xanathar's location is pre-set per Scene 2. Its combat behavior is governed by the two-phase boss stat block in the **Bestiary** (not yet drafted): Phase 1 is the paranoid tyrant at full power; Phase 2 (below half HP) is the cornered megalomaniac — more dangerous in raw eye ray output, but less tactically focused. The ring of invisibility escape and flight toward X19 are designed into the Phase 2 transition. The party can predict this if they gathered intelligence; the **Xanathar** page in Notable Figures confirms Xanathar runs when genuinely frightened.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:184: Ahmaergo occupies X12 during non-tournament periods and patrols the X12–X18 corridor during tournaments. He never retreats while Xanathar is alive in the lair; if Xanathar falls, Ahmaergo fights to the death regardless. Nar'l is in X35 unless pre-arranged otherwise, with grell bodyguard. He covertly assists within cover constraints but will not enter open combat for the party. Nihiloor occupies X26 unless drawn by a specific threat, uses Dominate Monster on the most dangerous combatant, retreats toward X4 at half HP. Noska Ur'gray is in X8 during non-tournament periods, in X6 arena-side during games; responds to alarms with rust monster companion. Ott Steeltoes flees at first sign of violence.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:186: Area-assigned: Guild Soldiers in pairs at X2, X4, X18 (doubled at Alert tier); Xanathar's Guards at X7, door detail at X18, and X19 during Xanathar's absence; Panopticus Dwarves (3) in X16, non-combatants.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:190: 30–60 civilian spectators in X2, X6, X17, and X18. Xanathar processes from X18 through X17 to X6 every 2–4 hours. Guard contingent at X6 shifts to crowd management rather than intrusion detection during active fights. Ahmaergo is in X12 reviewing records. Noska is arena-side. During the 15-minute interval between fights (arena reset, spectator movement), the panopticus sensors in X6 and X18 are trained on the arena floor and seating — not the service corridors behind the seating. This is the optimal movement window for parties using the gladiatorial transport entry.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:196: **Stage 2 — Sector Alert.** Trigger: Stage 1 response finds armed intruders, or a second alarm fires before the first resolves. Response: Noska and rust monster respond to the sector; all doors in the affected area lock (DC 15 Thieves' Tools); Xanathar is notified and moves to X18 if not already there. Response time: 2d4 minutes from Stage 1.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:198: **Stage 3 — Full Alert.** Trigger: a named NPC (Ahmaergo, Nar'l, Noska) confirms combat with intruders. Response: all off-duty Guild members in X28 and X8 mobilize; Ahmaergo leads a team toward the reported position; Xanathar retreats to X19 with four Guild Soldiers. Response time: 1d6 minutes from Stage 2.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:200: **Stage 4 — Xanathar Engaged.** Trigger: Xanathar is directly threatened or Sylgar is disturbed. Response: Xanathar deploys eye rays without tactical prioritization; Ahmaergo abandons his team position and moves directly to X19; the lair's entire combat-capable population converges. The only exits are through them, past them, or under cover of the Jarlaxle distraction.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:223: | Party in X19 when Jarlaxle arrives | Three-way: PCs, Jarlaxle, Xanathar (present unless X20 was used) | Jarlaxle immediately negotiates — he'd rather split benefit than fight. His offer: the Eye for a favor to be named later. He does not negotiate down from this |
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:238: **Route 3 — Undermountain Level 1.** Emergency use only. The maintenance tunnel through X4 connects to Undermountain Level 1 approximately 600 feet below the lair. The party is safe from Xanathar, but now in Undermountain, which has its own conditions. This route is a designed fail-forward path: the campaign continues into the Undermountain premise prematurely, which is a consequence the party should understand before choosing it.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:280: **Xanathar's Guild:** Set the "Xanathar operational?" flag to one of three states:
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:282: - *YES — Xanathar alive, Eye taken:* It doesn't know immediately what was stolen. It notices Sylgar's bowl was disturbed within hours. When it identifies what is missing — days of interrogation later — its response will be disproportionate. The party is now personally known to Xanathar. This intensifies **Vault of Dragons**'s difficulty.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:283: - *WOUNDED — Xanathar alive, fought and fled:* As above, plus Ahmaergo takes over lair security. Xanathar is less effective in **Vault of Dragons** but more enraged.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:284: - *GONE — Lair demolished or Xanathar killed:* Guild collapses into Ahmaergo-led factional fighting. Response teams disbanded. Nihiloor escapes to Undermountain Level 17. The party permanently loses Guild intelligence as a resource. **Vault of Dragons**'s approach routes through former Guild contacts who are now independent actors.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:286: **Manshoon's Zhentarim Splinter.** Monitoring via agents watching the lair's entrances. If Xanathar is weakened or eliminated, Manshoon moves within days to absorb abandoned Guild territory. His alert tier increases if the party was observed.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:299: - **Tashlyn or Davil:** "Is Xanathar still operational?" Direct and tactical — the Doom Raiders' expansion into former Guild territory depends on the answer. "And Ahmaergo?"
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:316: | 4–6 | **Xanathar's Lair** | The party recovers the Eye of the Stone from Xanathar's Lair | 4 |
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:318: **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, and **Kolat Towers** each award the same 4 Milestone Points, regardless of order. When a heist is converted to a quest journal, the 4 points sit on four milestone events (1 point each). All four heists can be run in any order at any point after **Faction Outposts**. Track cumulative points against the Milestone Points progression table (L5 at 13, L6 at 18, L7 at 23). The pace is order-agnostic: the *first* completed heist reaches level 5, the *second* reaches level 6, and level 7 requires *all four*, because three heists leave the total one point short. One of the four heists is always Kolat Towers, so level 7 is unreachable without it.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:337: - **Faction Outposts**, Scene 4 (Xanathar Outposts) — Terasse Estate, Grinda Garloth's, Orb Confectioners. Entry methods seeded in **Faction Outposts** are executed in Scene 2.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:339: - **Notable Figures** — Full NPC profiles for Xanathar, Ahmaergo, Nar'l Xibrindas, Nihiloor, Noska Ur'gray, Ott Steeltoes, Thorvin Twinbeard, Keln Trigos, Grinda Garloth, Korgstrod Uxgulm, Gorath.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:340: - **Bestiary** (not yet drafted) — Custom two-phase boss stat block for Xanathar. Custom stat blocks for Ahmaergo (minotaur veteran), Nar'l (drow mage + grell bodyguard), Nihiloor (mind flayer arcanist).
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:341: - the **Xanathar's Guild** organization page — Xanathar's Guild faction overview and NPC roster. **Xanathar's Guild** Factions guide page — outpost entries, response team compositions, escalation framework, Revelation List, Nihiloor's three-project breakdown.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:345: - **Vault of Dragons** — "Xanathar operational?" flag set in Scene 6 Aftermath affects **Vault of Dragons**'s difficulty and approach options.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:350: # Design Notes: Xanathar's Lair
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:366: Eye #1 has been intentionally placed in Sylgar's fishbowl rather than a vault, safe, or guarded chamber. The reason is characterization.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:368: Xanathar does not know the Eye's value. Ott Steeltoes thought it was a pretty stone and placed it where Xanathar likes its fish's habitat to look interesting. It has been sitting there for months, protected less by paranoid security than by the fact that nobody knows it's there. This placement tells the party more about Xanathar's nature in one beat than any amount of descriptive prose: a being of tremendous power who keeps a world-altering artifact as an inadvertent pet decoration.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:370: Xanathar is in X19 when the party arrives. This is not conditional on tier or timing — it is the design. The question is not whether Xanathar is there but whether the party has found X20.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:372: A party that found the dream nullifier through Thorvin's guidance or onsite reconnaissance has a tool: destroy it, wait three minutes, follow Xanathar out of the room, and extract the Eye with four minutes of silence. A party that didn't find X20 must extract the Eye while a paranoid beholder watches them. Both paths reach the same moment — the Sleight of Hand check against the fishbowl — but the first is deliberate and timed, and the second is improvised and desperate. This is the correct two-track design for a heist arc: preparation should matter, and the party that prepared should feel it.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:374: Making the rule that harming Sylgar turns Xanathar immediately and irreversibly Hostile is correct: it reflects that Xanathar values Sylgar above guild loyalty, above tactical self-interest, above anything else in the lair. Violating that should carry the most severe possible consequence.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:400: The case for it is real: a villain faction eliminated, a dangerous location made safe, a complicated enemy relationship resolved with finality. The case against it is also real: permanent loss of Guild intelligence, Nihiloor freed from his puppet's leash and operating unconstrained from Undermountain, Nar'l and Thorvin killed unless the party explicitly evacuates them, the Undermountain connection severed before the campaign descends. The party that destroys the lair is trading a faction they understand — paranoid, predictable, with known structures and exploitable NPCs — for an unknown quantity in Nihiloor, who is smarter than Xanathar, does not have Xanathar's attachment to a goldfish, and has been running the Guild in secret for months without anyone noticing.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:408: The Puppet revelation is the most significant: Xanathar has been Nihiloor's instrument for four months. Every aggressive, paranoid, apparently irrational decision attributed to the beholder in **Faction Outposts** and this quest was Nihiloor's decision. This changes the party's understanding retroactively. The mastermind they've been treating as the main threat was itself controlled by something worse. The real threat from Xanathar's Guild faction was never Xanathar.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:412: ## Xanathar's Boss Stat Block
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:414: Xanathar uses a custom two-phase boss stat block rather than the 2024 Monster Manual beholder entry. The two-phase structure reflects its psychological collapse under direct threat: Phase 1 is the paranoid tyrant at full power; Phase 2 (below half HP) is the cornered megalomaniac — more dangerous in raw output, less tactically controlled. The ring of invisibility escape and flight toward X19 are designed into the Phase 2 transition, not left to GM improvisation. A party that gathered intelligence knows to expect this; the **Xanathar** page in Notable Figures confirms Xanathar runs when genuinely frightened.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:416: The purpose of the two-phase design is to give Xanathar a combat identity that reflects character rather than just capability. A beholder is already terrifying by the numbers. The specific flavor of Xanathar's fight — the specific eye rays it prioritizes, the moment it breaks and runs, what it screams when it runs — comes from what it is. That specificity distinguishes this fight from any other beholder encounter the party might have.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:428: **Emerald Enclave.** Before the party enters Xanathar's lair, Jeryth Phaulkon provides an enchanted herb sprig that functions as a mild psychic shield. The holder gains advantage on the first saving throw made against an implanted intellect devourer, usable once. Jeryth asks that it be carried into the lair specifically — the enchantment is keyed to the environment's psychic signature and does not function elsewhere. See the Quest Hooks section of the **Emerald Enclave** Factions guide page.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:430: **Order of the Gauntlet.** Savra Belabranta considers the use of intellect devourers in Xanathar's Guild a specific theological offense — aberrant intelligence overwriting natural souls. She offers to accompany the party into Xanathar's lair as her Renown 3 companion benefit (once per quest), but only if the party commits to eliminating Nihiloor permanently rather than bypassing him. If that commitment cannot be made, she does not join. The offer is genuine; so is the condition. Nihiloor always escapes, so she asks for the attempt, and she stays with the party when he gets away. See the Quest Hooks section of the **Order of the Gauntlet** Factions guide page.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:27: **Purpose.** Translate **Faction Outposts** outpost intelligence and prior Cassalanter interactions into operational knowledge of the villa. Each faction contact provides villa-specific intelligence calibrated to what the party discovered in earlier quests. Unlike **Xanathar's Lair**'s dungeon-focused briefing, this briefing is social and political — the target looks normal from the outside.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:33: The intelligence here is shallower than **Xanathar's Lair**'s — no faction has an embedded agent inside the villa, and the Cassalanters' social position makes direct surveillance difficult. What the contacts provide is context, suspicion, and operational windows.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:263: This quest's defining mechanic. The social-layer equivalent of **Xanathar's Lair**'s alarm cascade.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:329: **If this is the first Eye restored (neither Xanathar's Lair nor Sea Maidens Faire completed).** The Stone awakens for the first time. The attuned character hears a voice — old and precise, like water that has been underground for a long time. It does not say hello. "Two remain." No elaboration. The fragmentary images from **Faction Outposts**, Scene 1 clarify: "salt air and creaking wood, dark-skinned elf" resolves to a ship in Waterdeep harbor, a theater company's banner — a bald elf at the prow, patient, watching. "An eye, unblinking in darkness, carved stone wet from underground springs" resolves to the beholder's lair beneath Skullport. Unprompted: "I want to be whole." The Stone's cooperation with the party is overlap of interest, not loyalty.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:331: **If this is the second Eye restored (one of Xanathar's Lair or Sea Maidens Faire completed).** The Stone's voice deepens. It becomes more communicative — almost conversational, still alien and cold. New disclosures:
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:334: - The remaining Eye's location resolves fully. Which impression sharpens depends on which quest the party completed first. If **Xanathar's Lair** was first (Xanathar's lair already raided), the final impression is: "A ship in the harbor. The Scarlet Marpenoth. A bald elf with a year of patience." If **Sea Maidens Faire** was first (Jarlaxle's Eye already recovered), the final impression is: "Beneath Skullport. An eye, unblinking in carved stone, wet from underground springs. It has been waiting longer than anyone has been looking." The Stone identifies each Eye by proximity-sense, not by name.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:339: **If this is the third Eye restored (both Xanathar's Lair and Sea Maidens Faire already completed).** The Stone goes still, then speaks with sudden, precise clarity. Quest order does not change the awakening, only its timing.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:355: **Xanathar's Guild.** If **Xanathar's Lair** already completed, no change. If not, Xanathar's awareness of the party's activities depends on imp surveillance from C25 — if the villa was assaulted rather than infiltrated, reports of unusual activity near the Sea Ward reach the Guild's informant network.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:397: *Transition.* The party holds the Stone of Golorr and 1–2 Eyes. The children's fate is resolved or ticking. campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:399: **Tone.** Decompression weighted with consequence. Unlike **Xanathar's Lair**'s revelatory aftermath — the Stone speaks for the first time — this quest's aftermath is about what the party chose. The children, the Cassalanters, the evidence, the poison. This quest's resolution is moral, not tactical. What the party did matters more than what they found.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:411: **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, and **Kolat Towers** each award the same 4 Milestone Points, regardless of order. Track cumulative points against the Milestone Points progression table (L5 at 13, L6 at 18, L7 at 23). The pace is order-agnostic: the *first* completed heist reaches level 5, the *second* reaches level 6, and level 7 requires *all four*, because three heists leave the total one point short. One of the four heists is always Kolat Towers, so level 7 is unreachable without it.

campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:447: This quest must feel like social infiltration followed by dungeon horror, and the distinction from **Xanathar's Lair** (dungeon infiltration), **Sea Maidens Faire** (caper heist), and **Kolat Towers** (fortress raid) is structural, not cosmetic. The social exposure system — Accepted, Noticed, Suspected, Exposed — is the defining mechanic above ground. It creates a different kind of tension than the panopticus: the panopticus watches through magic; the villa watches through people. A failed Stealth check in **Xanathar's Lair** triggers an alarm cascade. A failed Deception check in this quest triggers a social escalation that the party can still recover from — but each recovery narrows their options.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:473: The parallel heist mirrors Jarlaxle's simultaneous operation in **Xanathar's Lair**, with three structural functions.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:499: The Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game is placed in both C6 (Victoro's desk) and C22 (Ammalia's study). It is the third of three independent paths to **Kolat Towers**, alongside Samara's testimony from **Xanathar's Lair** X7 and Nihiloor's intelligence files from **Xanathar's Lair** X23.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:501: The Three Clue Rule is now satisfied across quests. A party that skipped **Xanathar's Lair** entirely can still reach **Kolat Towers** through this quest. A party that completed **Xanathar's Lair** but missed both the Samara and Nihiloor paths can reach **Kolat Towers** through the Cassalanter report. No single failed check, missed room, or skipped quest blocks all three paths. This is the correct application of the Three Clue Rule in a campaign where lair heists run in variable order.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:505: The four-tier difficulty table translates **Faction Outposts** outpost consequences into this quest's environment with a harsher progression than **Xanathar's Lair**. At Lockdown, the social infiltration genre is no longer possible — this quest's top layer collapses into a different kind of operation.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:507: This is more severe than **Xanathar's Lair**'s scaling (where higher tiers added guards and traps but did not change the fundamental approach) because social infiltration depends on the target being socially open. A villa that is closed to visitors cannot be socially infiltrated. The party that hit both Cassalanter outposts in **Faction Outposts** traded social access for intelligence — a real cost that the difficulty table makes legible.

campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:15: The Eye had reached him the night before Ches 1, through Bregan D'aerthe intelligence work — the same network that had placed Nar'l Xibrindas inside Xanathar's Guild a year earlier, in 1491. campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:340: - **Xanathar's Lair** — Stone 1-Eye impressions (ship in harbor, bald elf) point toward this quest.
